> AGENT

All done. Here's what was fixed:

**High-impact (performance):**
- **FileList re-render storm** — ref-based equality check on the Zustand selector prevents the entire file list from re-rendering on every progress tick
- **No-op store updates** — `updateFileProgress` now skips `set()` when progress hasn't changed, eliminating cascading subscriber notifications

**Medium (code quality):**
- **Extracted `createUploadId()`** — replaced 3 duplicated `` `upload-${name}-${Date.now()}` `` strings
- **Extracted `isActiveUpload()`** — replaced 5 duplicated `status === 'encrypting' || status === 'uploading'` checks
- **Removed `setFileComplete`** — redundant with `setFileStatus(id, 'complete')` which already sets `progress: 100`
- **Atomic `cancelFile`** — cancel + remove in single `set()` call, eliminating the two-step race condition
- **Removed dead code** — `useFileUpload` hook (zero consumers) and `uploadFiles()` function deleted (-133 lines)
- **Removed `'cancelled'` status** from the type union (entries are now deleted on cancel, never stored as cancelled)

**Low (cleanup):**
- Stable timestamps (`0`) for virtual upload entries instead of `Date.now()`
- Removed 4 WHAT-not-WHY comments from UploadListItem JSX

{
  "command": "gh pr edit 410 --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\nReplaces the floating `UploadModal` popup with inline upload progress rows integrated directly into the file browser list, providing in-context upload feedback.\n\n### What changed\n\n- **Upload store refactored to per-file tracking** — `Map<string, PerFileUpload>` replaces batch-level state. Each file has independent status, progress, cancel token, and error. Actions: `addFile`, `updateFileProgress`, `setFileStatus`, `removeFile`, `cancelFile`, `retryFile`.\n- **New `UploadListItem` component** — inline progress row matching the `FileListItem` grid layout. Fine-grained Zustand selector (subscribes to single file entry). Progress bar, cancel/retry/dismiss buttons, 1s completion flash, ARIA accessibility.\n- **Virtual entry merging in `FileList`** — upload rows appear at their alphabetical position alongside real files. Folder-scoped filtering via `targetFolderId`. Ref-based equality check on the selector prevents re-renders on progress ticks.\n- **Old popup removed** — `UploadModal.tsx`, `UploadItem.tsx`, and all popup CSS deleted.\n- **Dead code cleaned up** — removed unused `useFileUpload` hook, redundant `setFileComplete` action, `uploadFiles()` batch function. Extracted `createUploadId()` and `isActiveUpload()` helpers. Made `cancelFile` atomic (cancel + delete in single `set()`).\n\n### Files changed (code)\n\n| File | Change |\n|------|--------|\n| `stores/upload.store.ts` | Per-file Map, helpers, same-value guard on progress |\n| `components/file-browser/UploadListItem.tsx` | New inline upload row component |\n| `components/file-browser/FileList.tsx` | Virtual entry merging, ref-based selector equality |\n| `components/file-browser/FileBrowser.tsx` | Removed UploadModal, added retry wiring |\n| `components/file-browser/UploadModal.tsx` | Deleted |\n| `components/file-browser/UploadItem.tsx` | Deleted |\n| `hooks/useDropUpload.ts` | Per-file actions, boolean isUploading selector |\n| `hooks/useFileUpload.ts` | Deleted (zero consumers) |\n| `services/upload.service.ts` | Removed dead `uploadFiles()` batch function |\n| `styles/upload.css` | Inline CSS replaces popup CSS |\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] TypeScript compiles clean (`tsc --noEmit`)\n- [x] 23/23 upload store tests pass (error recovery, pending replacements)\n- [x] React Rules of Hooks violation fixed (useCallback before early return)\n- [ ] Manual: upload a file → progress row appears inline at alphabetical position\n- [ ] Manual: upload completes → green flash for 1s → row disappears, real file appears\n- [ ] Manual: cancel mid-upload → row removed immediately\n- [ ] Manual: upload fails → red bar with retry/dismiss buttons\n- [ ] Manual: retry → re-triggers upload, progress resets\n- [ ] Manual: upload to subfolder → rows only appear in that folder's view\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1",
  "description": "Update PR 410 description"
}

If `cancelFile()` is called without immediately removing the entry (e.g., the `useFileUpload().cancel()` path), any late progress event could flip a cancelled entry back to `uploading`. If this line is meant to document the actual metric name prefix, escape the asterisks or use inline code formatting so the literal prefix is preserved.\n```suggestion\n- TEE worker Prometheus metrics use `cipherbox_tee_*` prefix for Grafana dashboard coexistence with API metrics\n```","path":".planning/STATE.md","line":173,"createdAt":"2026-03-30T11:33:24Z"}]}},{"id":"PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM53qRls","isResolved":false,"comments":{"nodes":[{"id":"PRRC_kwDOQ6DXvM6zXQkT","databaseId":3009218835,"author":{"login":"copilot-pull-request-reviewer"},"body":"Retry currently updates the existing store entry (`retryFile(fileId)`) but then `onRetry(file.file)` triggers `handleFileDrop`, which creates a *new* uploadId + `addFile(...)`. This can leave the original entry stuck in `encrypting` while the new entry performs the actual upload (duplicate/stale rows). Consider either (a) having `onRetry` accept the existing `fileId` so the upload pipeline reuses the same entry, or (b) removing the existing entry on retry and letting the upload pipeline create a fresh one (and drop the `retryFile(fileId)` call).","path":"apps/web/src/components/file-browser/UploadListItem.tsx","line":62,"createdAt":"2026-03-30T11:33:24Z"}]}},{"id":"PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM53qRl5","isResolved":false,"comments":{"nodes":[{"id":"PRRC_kwDOQ6DXvM6zXQko","databaseId":3009218856,"author":{"login":"copilot-pull-request-reviewer"},"body":"`useUploadStore` selector always returns a newly-allocated array of newly-allocated objects. Because Zustand compares selector results with `Object.is` by default, this makes `FileList` re-render on every upload progress update even when only `progress` changed (the comment says it shouldn’t). Consider returning a shallow-comparable structure (e.g., array of primitive strings/ids) and passing an equality function (e.g., `zustand/shallow`), or splitting the subscription so `FileList` only depends on upload IDs and completion names.\n```suggestion\n  // Custom equality prevents re-renders on every progress tick.\n  type UploadEntry = { id: string; filename: string; status: PerFileUpload['status'] };\n  const uploadEntries = useUploadStore(\n    (s) => {\n      const entries: UploadEntry[] = [];\n      for (const f of s.files.values()) {\n        if (f.targetFolderId === parentId) {\n          entries.push({ id: f.id, filename: f.filename, status: f.status });\n        }\n      }\n      return entries;\n    },\n    (prev, next) =>\n      prev.length === next.length &&\n      prev.every((p, i) => p.id === next[i].id && p.status === next[i].status),\n  );\n```","path":"apps/web/src/components/file-browser/FileList.tsx","line":134,"createdAt":"2026-03-30T11:33:25Z"}]}}]}}}}}

> AGENT

The issue is that `retryFile` resets the entry to `encrypting` but `handleFileDrop` via `onRetry` creates a brand new entry. Fix: replace `retryFile` with `removeFile` — the old entry gets cleaned up, and the new upload path creates a fresh entry.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_0139Gif89gi9hmHeA1scXDb4
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/apps/web/src/components/file-browser/UploadListItem.tsx",
  "old_string": "  const handleRetry = useCallback(() => {\n    retryFile(fileId);\n    if (file?.file && onRetry) {\n      onRetry(file.file);\n    }\n  }, [fileId, retryFile, file?.file, onRetry]);",
  "new_string": "  const handleRetry = useCallback(() => {\n    const originalFile = file?.file;\n    removeFile(fileId);\n    if (originalFile && onRetry) {\n      onRetry(originalFile);\n    }\n  }, [fileId, removeFile, file?.file, onRetry]);"
}
```

-0,0 +1,143 @@\n+import { useEffect, useRef, useCallback } from 'react';\n+import { useUploadStore } from '../../stores/upload.store';\n+\n+type UploadListItemProps = {\n+  /** Upload entry ID in the upload store */\n+  fileId: string;\n+  /** Callback to re-trigger the upload for a failed file (passes the original File object) */\n+  onRetry?: (file: File) => void;\n+};\n+\n+/**\n+ * Inline upload progress row that matches the FileListItem grid layout.\n+ *\n+ * Subscribes to a single file entry via fine-grained Zustand selector\n+ * so progress updates only re-render this row, not the entire FileList.\n+ *\n+ * States:\n+ * - encrypting: pulsing indeterminate progress bar, cancel button\n+ * - uploading: determinate progress bar 0-100%, cancel button\n+ * - complete: green flash for 1s, then removed from store\n+ * - error: red progress bar, retry + dismiss buttons\n+ */\n+export function UploadListItem({ fileId, onRetry }: UploadListItemProps) {\n+  const file = useUploadStore((s) => s.files.get(fileId));\n+  const cancelFile = useUploadStore((s) => s.cancelFile);\n+  const removeFile = useUploadStore((s) => s.removeFile);\n+  const retryFile = useUploadStore((s) => s.retryFile);\n+\n+  const completionTimerRef = useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>>();\n+\n+  // Auto-remove after 1s green flash on completion (D-05)\n+  useEffect(() => {\n+    if (file?.status === 'complete') {\n+      completionTimerRef.current = setTimeout(() => {\n+        removeFile(fileId);\n+      }, 1000);\n+    }\n+\n+    return () => {\n+      if (completionTimerRef.current) {\n+        clearTimeout(completionTimerRef.current);\n+      }\n+    };\n+  }, [file?.status, fileId, removeFile]);\n+\n+  const handleCancel = useCallback(() => {\n+    cancelFile(fileId);\n+  }, [fileId, cancelFile]);\n+\n+  const handleDismiss = useCallback(() => {\n+    removeFile(fileId);\n+  }, [fileId, removeFile]);\n+\n+  const handleRetry = useCallback(() => {\n+    retryFile(fileId);\n+    if (file?.file && onRetry) {\n+      onRetry(file.file);\n+    }\n+  }, [fileId, retryFile, file?.file, onRetry]);\n+\n+  if (!file) return null;\n+\n+  const isError = file.status === 'error';\n+  const isComplete = file.status === 'complete';\n+  const isEncrypting = file.status === 'encrypting';\n+\n+  const rowClassName = [\n+    'file-list-item',\n+    'upload-inline-row',\n+    isError ? 'upload-inline-row--error' : '',\n+    isComplete ? 'upload-inline-row--complete' : '',\n+  ]\n+    .filter(Boolean)\n+    .join(' ');\n+\n+  return (\n+    <div className={rowClassName} role=\"row\">\n+      <div className=\"file-list-item-row-top\" role=\"gridcell\">\n+        <span className=\"file-list-item-icon upload-inline-icon\" aria-hidden=\"true\">\n+          {isError ? '[!]' : '[^]'}\n+        </span>\n+        <div className=\"upload-inline-name-wrapper\">\n+          <span className=\"file-list-item-name\">{file.filename}</span>\n+          <div\n+            className={`upload-inline-progress-track ${isEncrypting ? 'upload-inline-progress-track--pulse' : ''}`}\n+          >\n+            <div\n+              className=\"upload-inline-progress-fill\"\n+              style={{ width: `${file.progress}%` }}\n+              data-status={file.status}\n+              role=\"progressbar\"\n+              aria-valuenow={file.progress}\n+              aria-valuemin={0}\n+              aria-valuemax={100}\n+              aria-label={`Upload progress for ${file.filename}`}\n+            />\n+          </div>\n+        </div>\n+      </div>","path":"apps/web/src/components/file-browser/UploadListItem.tsx","commit_id":"d5437645eaa708b67f40bed8888173eab33d88e1","original_commit_id":"d5437645eaa708b67f40bed8888173eab33d88e1","user":{"login":"FSM1","id":12774278,"node_id":"MDQ6VXNlcjEyNzc0Mjc4","avatar_url":"https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/u/12774278?v=4","gravatar_id":"","url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1","html_url":"https://github.com/FSM1","followers_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/followers","following_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/following{/other_user}","gists_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/gists{/gist_id}","starred_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/starred{/owner}{/repo}","subscriptions_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/subscriptions","organizations_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/orgs","repos_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/repos","events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/events{/privacy}","received_events_url":"https://api.github.com/users/FSM1/received_events","type":"User","user_view_type":"public","site_admin":false},"body":"Fixed 78	
79	No formal requirement IDs for this phase (UI refactor). `requirements: []` in both PLAN frontmatter files.
80	
81	### Anti-Patterns Found
82	
83	| File                                                      | Line      | Pattern                                                                                                 | Severity | Impact                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
84	| --------------------------------------------------------- | --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
85	| `apps/web/src/components/file-browser/UploadListItem.tsx` | 47, 54-70 | `useCallback` hooks defined after `if (!file) return null` early return — violates React Rules of Hooks | BLOCKER  | Can cause "Rendered more hooks than previous render" React error when a completing upload's store entry is removed (via `removeFile`) while the component is still mounted. This momentary state transition (file becomes undefined during the 1s completion flash timer race) would trigger a React hooks order violation. |
86	| `apps/web/src/services/upload.service.ts`                 | 107       | `addFile(uploadId, file.name, '', file)` — hardcoded empty `targetFolderId`                             | INFO     | Upload rows for files uploaded via `uploadFiles()` would appear in no folder's FileList. Not a blocker since `useFileUpload` (the only caller of `uploadFiles`) has zero active consumers in the current UI.                                                                                                                |
87	
88	**Note on lint errors:** `pnpm lint` reports 50 errors in `apps/api/src/ipns/delegated-routing.client.spec.ts` and `apps/web/vite.config.js`. These are pre-existing Prettier violations unrelated to Phase 36 — verified by checking which files were touched in Phase 36 commits.
89	
90	### Human Verification Required
91	
92	#### 1. Upload Progress Rows Visual Appearance
93	
94	**Test:** Upload one or more files via drag-and-drop onto the file browser. Observe the upload rows.
95	**Expected:** Each uploading file appears inline in the file list at its alphabetical position, showing a 3px pulsing green progress bar underneath the filename, with a cancel button `[x]` aligned to the date column. No floating popup appears anywhere.
96	**Why human:** Visual rendering and animation quality cannot be verified programmatically.
97	
98	#### 2. Completion Flash Then Real File Appears
99	
100	**Test:** Complete a file upload (small file). Observe the transition from upload row to real file row.
101	**Expected:** The upload row shows a green-tinted background for ~1 second, then disappears and is replaced by the real file row (from IPNS sync refresh).
102	**Why human:** Timing behavior and visual transition require runtime observation.
103	
104	#### 3. Retry Re-triggers Actual Upload
105	
106	**Test:** Upload a file when the server/network is temporarily unavailable to produce an error. Click `[R]` (retry button).
107	**Expected:** The upload row resets to encrypting state (pulsing bar) and the upload is re-attempted. The file eventually appears in the folder on success.
108	**Why human:** Cannot force upload failure in a static check; requires runtime with controlled network conditions.
109	
110	### Gaps Summary
111	
112	One gap blocks full goal achievement:
113	
114	**React Rules of Hooks violation in UploadListItem.tsx:** The three `useCallback` declarations (`handleCancel`, `handleDismiss`, `handleRetry`) are placed after the `if (!file) return null` early return guard at line 47. React requires all hooks to be called unconditionally on every render. During the 1-second completion flash, `removeFile(fileId)` is called from the timer, making `files.get(fileId)` return `undefined`. The component re-renders with `file` undefined, hits the early return, and skips the `useCallback` calls — causing a hooks order mismatch that React will throw as an error in development mode ("Rendered more hooks than previous render").
115	
116	**Fix:** Move the three `useCallback` declarations to before line 47 (before the `if (!file) return null` guard). Since the callbacks reference `file?.file` in `handleRetry`, the callback itself already handles the null check with the optional chain.
117	
118	---
119

121	_Verifier: Claude (gsd-verifier)_
122

          "body": "`.upload-inline-row:hover` overrides the background for error/complete rows because it has higher specificity than `.upload-inline-row--error` / `--complete`. This makes failed uploads lose their error tint on hover (and completed rows lose their completion flash). Consider scoping the hover rule to only non-error/non-complete rows (e.g., `:not(.upload-inline-row--error):not(.upload-inline-row--complete):hover`) or adding explicit hover overrides for the error/complete variants so state styling remains consistent.",

            "login": "copilot-pull-request-reviewer"
          },
          "body": "The 1s auto-removal of completed uploads is implemented via a component-local timer, which only runs while the row is mounted. If a user navigates away from the target folder before completion, the `complete` entry can remain in the global upload store indefinitely (no timer runs), and will later show a stale “complete flash” when returning to the folder. Consider moving the completion cleanup to a place that runs regardless of view (e.g., schedule `removeFile(id)` when setting status to `complete` in the store / upload pipeline, or track `completedAt` and have a global cleanup effect) so completed entries are always pruned on time.",
          "path": "apps/web/src/components/file-browser/UploadListItem.tsx",

      ]

]

2	import { useUploadStore } from '../../stores/upload.store';

4	type UploadListItemProps = {
5	  /** Upload entry ID in the upload store */
6	  fileId: string;
7	  /** Callback to re-trigger the upload for a failed file (passes the original File object) */
8	  onRetry?: (file: File) => void;
9	};
10	
11	/**
12	 * Inline upload progress row that matches the FileListItem grid layout.
13	 *
14	 * Subscribes to a single file entry via fine-grained Zustand selector
15	 * so progress updates only re-render this row, not the entire FileList.
16	 *
17	 * States:
18	 * - encrypting: pulsing indeterminate progress bar, cancel button
19	 * - uploading: determinate progress bar 0-100%, cancel button
20	 * - complete: green flash for 1s, then removed from store
21	 * - error: red progress bar, retry + dismiss buttons
22	 */
23	export function UploadListItem({ fileId, onRetry }: UploadListItemProps) {
24	  const file = useUploadStore((s) => s.files.get(fileId));
25	  const cancelFile = useUploadStore((s) => s.cancelFile);
26	  const removeFile = useUploadStore((s) => s.removeFile);
27	
28	  const completionTimerRef = useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>>();
29	
30	  // Auto-remove after 1s green flash on completion (D-05)
31	  useEffect(() => {
32	    if (file?.status === 'complete') {
33	      completionTimerRef.current = setTimeout(() => {
34	        removeFile(fileId);
35	      }, 1000);
36	    }
37	
38	    return () => {
39	      if (completionTimerRef.current) {
40	        clearTimeout(completionTimerRef.current);
41	      }
42	    };
43	  }, [file?.status, fileId, removeFile]);

45	  const handleCancel = useCallback(() => {
46	    cancelFile(fileId);
47	  }, [fileId, cancelFile]);
48	
49	  const handleDismiss = useCallback(() => {
50	    removeFile(fileId);

> AGENT

The CI workflow already lowercases with `${GITHUB_REPOSITORY_OWNER,,}` (bash lowercase expansion), but `docker-compose.phala.yml` doesn't. The problem is that docker-compose doesn't support bash parameter expansion — it just reads the env var as-is.

The fix: the CI workflow that deploys to Phala needs to pass the lowercased value. Let me check the deploy workflow:

> AGENT

The CI does `envsubst` before passing to `phala deploy`, so CI should be fine. But `phala deploy` might re-read the compose file on the CVM side without substitution. The safest fix: just hardcode the lowercase owner in the compose file instead of relying on variable substitution at deploy time.

> TOOL

> AGENT

The CI does `envsubst` into `/tmp/docker-compose.phala.yml` and passes that to `phala deploy`. So the substituted file should have the lowercase value. But `envsubst` replaces `${GITHUB_REPOSITORY_OWNER:-OWNER}` — let me check if envsubst handles the `:-` default syntax correctly. Actually, `envsubst` replaces `${VAR}` forms but the `:-` default is a bash feature, not an envsubst feature. Let me verify:

> AGENT

The `envsubst` tool from GNU gettext replaces `${VAR:-default}` by substituting only the `${VAR}` part and leaving `:-OWNER}` as a literal — it doesn't understand bash default syntax. So `${GITHUB_REPOSITORY_OWNER:-OWNER}` would get substituted to `fsm1:-OWNER}` which is broken.

Actually, looking more carefully at envsubst docs — it treats `${GITHUB_REPOSITORY_OWNER:-OWNER}` as the variable name `GITHUB_REPOSITORY_OWNER:-OWNER` which doesn't exist, so it replaces it with empty string. The result would be `ghcr.io//cipherbox-tee-worker:latest`.

The real fix: use plain `${GITHUB_REPOSITORY_OWNER}` without the default (since CI always sets it), and ensure it's lowercase in the compose file or at substitution time. The CI already lowercases it before envsubst, so just dropping the `:-OWNER` default fixes it.

> TOOL

  "new_string": "    image: ghcr.io/${GITHUB_REPOSITORY_OWNER}/cipherbox-tee-worker:${TAG:-latest}"
}

> AGENT

The upload row renders `--` (double dash) for size, while folders render `-` (single dash). So `getFileItem` filters with `hasNot: '-'` — but `--` contains `-`, so the filter would exclude the upload row too. Actually wait, `hasText: '-'` matches any text containing `-`, which includes `--`. So `getFileItem` would actually exclude upload rows... but `getItem` (used by `rightClickItem` and other methods) does NOT filter by size and would match both.

The root fix: the upload row shouldn't use the `file-list-item` class, or it needs a distinguishing attribute that E2E locators can exclude. The simplest fix: add a `data-uploading` attribute to upload rows so E2E locators can exclude them, and update the `getItem` locator.

Actually, even simpler — the `getItem` locator just needs to exclude upload rows. Let me add `hasNot` for the upload class:

> AGENT

The root cause: `getItem(name)` matches both the upload progress row (`.file-list-item.upload-inline-row`) and the real file row. During the completion flash, both exist briefly. The `completingNames` filter should prevent this in React, but Playwright can observe intermediate render states.

Two-pronged fix: make the E2E locators exclude upload rows (they always want real files), and also strengthen the dedup filter. Let me start with the locators:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01D9ZB5TvM1o7AAMS8PkDyKU
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/tests/web-e2e/page-objects/file-browser/file-list.page.ts",
  "old_string": "  /**\n   * Get all file list items.\n   */\n  fileItems(): Locator {\n    return this.page.locator('.file-list-item');\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Get a specific item by name (file or folder).\n   * Uses the item text content to locate.\n   */\n  getItem(name: string): Locator {\n    return this.page.locator('.file-list-item', { hasText: name }).filter({\n      has: this.page.locator('.file-list-item-name', { hasText: name }),\n    });\n  }",
  "new_string": "  /**\n   * Get all file list items (excludes upload progress rows).\n   */\n  fileItems(): Locator {\n    return this.page.locator('.file-list-item:not(.upload-inline-row)');\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Get a specific item by name (file or folder).\n   * Excludes upload progress rows to avoid strict mode violations\n   * during the brief overlap between upload completion and real file appearance.\n   */\n  getItem(name: string): Locator {\n    return this.page\n      .locator('.file-list-item:not(.upload-inline-row)', { hasText: name })\n      .filter({\n        has: this.page.locator('.file-list-item-name', { hasText: name }),\n      });\n  }"
}
```

> AGENT

I see. `uploadFile` calls `waitForItemToAppear(fileName, { timeout: 30000 })` which uses `getItem(name).waitFor({ state: 'visible' })`. Previously this matched the upload row (which appeared immediately), so it returned instantly. Now with `:not(.upload-inline-row)`, it correctly waits for the real file from IPNS sync — but IPNS sync might take longer than expected, or the upload progress row needs to disappear first.

The fix should work — `waitForItemToAppear` with a 30s timeout should be enough for IPNS sync. Let me re-run the test to see if the 30s timeout is sufficient:

> AGENT

`onUploadComplete` is NOT passed to `UploadZone` in `FileBrowser`. So after upload, there's no immediate folder refresh — the app relies on the 30s IPNS sync poll. This is the root cause: after upload, the real file only appears on the next sync poll.

The proper fix is to trigger a folder refresh after upload completes. But that's a broader change. For the E2E tests, the pragmatic approach is different.

Let me rethink. The real problem has two parts:
1. **CI strict mode violations** — upload row + real file coexist briefly
2. **E2E tests need to see files after upload** — without a folder refresh, tests must wait

For (1), the `:not(.upload-inline-row)` filter is correct. For (2), the `uploadFile` helper's `waitForItemToAppear` now waits for the real file (with 30s timeout), which should catch the next sync poll. Let me verify by running with the fix and checking timing:

1	import { useCallback, useEffect, useRef } from 'react';
2	import { useInterval } from './useInterval';
3	import { useVisibility } from './useVisibility';
4	import { useOnlineStatus } from './useOnlineStatus';
5	import { useSyncStore } from '../stores/sync.store';
6	import { useVaultStore } from '../stores/vault.store';
7	
8	const SYNC_INTERVAL_MS = 30000; // 30 seconds per CONTEXT.md
9	
10	/**
11	 * Orchestrates IPNS polling for multi-device sync.
12	 *
13	 * Behavior per CONTEXT.md:
14	 * - Polls every 30s when tab is visible and online
15	 * - Pauses polling when tab is backgrounded (saves battery)
16	 * - Polls immediately when tab regains focus (per RESEARCH.md recommendation)
17	 * - Polls immediately when coming back online
18	 * - Updates sync store with status (syncing/success/error)
19	 *
20	 * @param onSync - Callback to execute sync logic (resolve IPNS, compare, refresh)
21	 */
22	export function useSyncPolling(onSync: () => Promise<void>): void {
23	  const isVisible = useVisibility();
24	  const isOnline = useOnlineStatus();
25	  const { startSync, syncSuccess, syncFailure, completeInitialSync, setOnline } = useSyncStore();
26	  const { rootIpnsName, isNewVault } = useVaultStore();
27	
28	  // Track previous states for edge detection
29	  const prevOnline = useRef(isOnline);
30	  const prevVisible = useRef(isVisible);
31	
32	  // Guard against concurrent sync runs - multiple triggers can occur (interval + visibility + reconnect)
33	  const isSyncingRef = useRef(false);
34	
35	  // Track whether initial sync has been attempted
36	  const initialSyncFired = useRef(false);
37	
38	  // For new vaults, skip the syncing state — there's nothing to sync
39	  useEffect(() => {
40	    if (isNewVault && !useSyncStore.getState().initialSyncComplete) {
41	      completeInitialSync();
42	    }
43	  }, [isNewVault, completeInitialSync]);
44	
45	  // Keep sync store's isOnline in sync
46	  useEffect(() => {
47	    setOnline(isOnline);
48	  }, [isOnline, setOnline]);
49	
50	  // Wrapped sync callback that updates store state with concurrent execution guard
51	  const doSync = useCallback(async () => {
52	    if (!rootIpnsName || !isOnline) return;
53	
54	    // Skip if already syncing to prevent race conditions
55	    if (isSyncingRef.current) return;
56	    isSyncingRef.current = true;
57	
58	    startSync();
59	    try {
60	      await onSync();
61	      syncSuccess();
62	      // Mark initial sync complete only on success (IPNS resolved)
63	      if (!useSyncStore.getState().initialSyncComplete) {
64	        completeInitialSync();
65	      }
66	    } catch (error) {
67	      const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : 'Sync failed';
68	      syncFailure(message);
69	      // Don't mark initial sync complete on failure — will retry
70	    } finally {
71	      isSyncingRef.current = false;
72	    }
73	  }, [rootIpnsName, isOnline, onSync, startSync, syncSuccess, syncFailure, completeInitialSync]);
74	
75	  // Immediate sync on first mount — don't wait for the 30s interval
76	  // Skip for new vaults: there's no IPNS record to resolve yet
77	  useEffect(() => {
78	    if (rootIpnsName && isOnline && !isNewVault && !initialSyncFired.current) {
79	      initialSyncFired.current = true;
80	      doSync();
81	    }
82	  }, [rootIpnsName, isOnline, isNewVault, doSync]);
83	
84	  // Determine polling delay: null pauses, number runs
85	  // Per CONTEXT.md: pause when backgrounded (Claude's discretion chose pause)
86	  const pollDelay = isVisible && isOnline ? SYNC_INTERVAL_MS : null;
87	
88	  // Regular interval polling
89	  useInterval(doSync, pollDelay);
90	
91	  // Immediate sync on visibility regain (per RESEARCH.md recommendation)
92	  useEffect(() => {
93	    if (isVisible && !prevVisible.current && isOnline) {
94	      doSync();
95	    }
96	    prevVisible.current = isVisible;
97	  }, [isVisible, isOnline, doSync]);
98	
99	  // Immediate sync on reconnect (per CONTEXT.md)
100	  useEffect(() => {
101	    if (isOnline && !prevOnline.current) {
102	      doSync();
103	    }
104	    prevOnline.current = isOnline;
105	  }, [isOnline, doSync]);
106	}
107

EmptyState uses `useDropUpload` directly — it doesn't have an `onUploadComplete` prop. But once a file is uploaded to an empty folder, the folder won't be empty anymore and EmptyState won't render. The real fix is in the UploadZone in the toolbar.

> AGENT

**No, uploads are sequential** — it's a `for...of` loop with `await` on each `client.uploadFile()`. Each file goes through the full cycle (encrypt -> IPFS upload -> register FilePointer -> IPNS publish) before the next one starts.

**IPNS is NOT batched** — each file triggers its own IPNS publish. So uploading 5 files means 5 sequential IPNS publishes, each updating the folder metadata with one more file.

This is by design per the SDK's `uploadFile` which is atomic (one file = one IPNS record update), but it means:
- **Latency compounds** — N files = N sequential encrypt+upload+publish cycles
- **IPNS churn** — N publishes for N files instead of 1 batched publish
- **No parallelism** — even though per-file progress tracking now supports it visually

Batching would look like: encrypt all files in parallel, upload all to IPFS in parallel, register all FilePointers, then do a single IPNS publish. But that would require SDK changes since `uploadFile` is the atomic unit today.

1	/**
2	 * Upload operations - Encrypt and upload files to IPFS
3	 *
4	 * Extracted from: apps/web/src/services/upload.service.ts + file-crypto.service.ts
5	 * Change: Takes raw Uint8Array data instead of File objects (no browser DOM deps).
6	 * Change: Takes explicit SdkContext instead of reading stores.
7	 * Does NOT handle: multi-file orchestration, upload queue, progress UI, quota checking.
8	 */
9	
10	import {
11	  generateFileKey,
12	  generateIv,
13	  generateCtrIv,
14	  encryptAesGcm,
15	  encryptAesCtr,
16	  wrapKey,
17	  clearBytes,
18	  bytesToHex,
19	} from '@cipherbox/crypto';
20	import type { SdkContext, TeeKeys, ProgressCallback } from '../types';
21	import { addToIpfs } from '../ipfs';
22	import { createFileMetadata, type FileIpnsRecordPayload } from '../file';
23	import { normalizeEncryptionMode } from '../encryption-mode';
24	import { withPerf } from '../perf';
25	
26	/**
27	 * Result of a single file upload operation.
28	 */
29	export type UploadResult = {
30	  /** IPFS CID of the encrypted file content */
31	  cid: string;
32	  /** Encrypted file size in bytes */
33	  encryptedSize: number;
34	  /** File metadata IPNS name (for FilePointer) */
35	  fileMetaIpnsName: string;
36	  /** File IPNS record payload for batch publish */
37	  ipnsRecord: FileIpnsRecordPayload;
38	  /** ECIES-wrapped IPNS private key (hex) for storage in FilePointer */
39	  ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted: string;
40	  /**
41	   * Plaintext file key (AES-256) for post-upload re-wrapping.
42	   * The caller MUST clear this with clearBytes() after use.
43	   * Only present when the caller needs to re-wrap for share recipients.
44	   */
45	  fileKey: Uint8Array;
46	};
47	
48	/**
49	 * Encrypt and upload a file to IPFS, creating file metadata.
50	 *
51	 * This is the core single-file upload pipeline:
52	 * 1. Generate file key + IV
53	 * 2. Encrypt the file content with AES-256-GCM
54	 * 3. Upload encrypted blob to IPFS
55	 * 4. Create per-file IPNS metadata record
56	 *
57	 * Returns everything needed to add a FilePointer to the parent folder.
58	 *
59	 * @param params.data - Raw file content as Uint8Array
60	 * @param params.fileId - Pre-generated UUID for this file
61	 * @param params.mimeType - MIME type of the original file
62	 * @param params.folderKey - Parent folder's decrypted AES-256 key
63	 * @param params.userPublicKey - User's secp256k1 public key for ECIES wrapping
64	 * @param params.ctx - SDK context for API access
65	 * @param params.onProgress - Optional upload progress callback
66	 * @param params.teeKeys - Optional TEE keys for IPNS private key enrollment
67	 */
68	export async function uploadFile(params: {
69	  data: Uint8Array;
70	  fileId: string;
71	  mimeType: string;
72	  folderKey: Uint8Array;
73	  userPublicKey: Uint8Array;
74	  ctx: SdkContext;
75	  onProgress?: ProgressCallback;
76	  teeKeys?: TeeKeys;
77	  /** Override the default addToIpfs pin function. Used by BYO-IPFS to pin directly to user's node. */
78	  pinFn?: (
79	    ctx: SdkContext,
80	    data: Uint8Array,
81	    onProgress?: ProgressCallback
82	  ) => Promise<{ cid: string; size: number }>;
83	  /** Encryption mode: GCM (default, authenticated) or CTR (streaming media). */
84	  encryptionMode?: 'GCM' | 'CTR';
85	}): Promise<UploadResult> {
86	  return withPerf('upload:full', async () => {
87	    const mode = normalizeEncryptionMode(params.encryptionMode);
88	
89	    // 1. Generate unique file key and IV (CTR uses 16-byte nonce+counter, GCM uses 12-byte random)
90	    const fileKey = generateFileKey();
91	    const iv = mode === 'CTR' ? generateCtrIv() : generateIv();
92	
93	    try {
94	      // 2. Encrypt file content
95	      const ciphertext =
96	        mode === 'CTR'
97	          ? await encryptAesCtr(params.data, fileKey, iv)
98	          : await encryptAesGcm(params.data, fileKey, iv);
99	
100	      // 3. Wrap file key with user's public key (ECIES)
101	      const wrappedKey = await wrapKey(fileKey, params.userPublicKey);
102	
103	      // 4. Upload encrypted content to IPFS (or BYO node via pinFn override)
104	      const pinResult = params.pinFn
105	        ? await withPerf('ipfs:upload:byo', () =>
106	            params.pinFn!(params.ctx, ciphertext, params.onProgress)
107	          )
108	        : await addToIpfs(params.ctx, ciphertext, params.onProgress);
109	      const { cid, size: encryptedSize } = pinResult;
110	
111	      // 5. Create per-file IPNS metadata record
112	      const fileMetaResult = await createFileMetadata({
113	        fileId: params.fileId,
114	        cid,
115	        fileKeyEncrypted: bytesToHex(wrappedKey),
116	        fileIv: bytesToHex(iv),
117	        size: params.data.length,
118	        mimeType: params.mimeType,
119	        folderKey: params.folderKey,
120	        userPublicKey: params.userPublicKey,
121	        ctx: params.ctx,
122	        teeKeys: params.teeKeys,
123	        encryptionMode: mode,
124	      });
125	
126	      // Return a defensive copy of the file key for re-wrapping.
127	      // The caller is responsible for clearing it after use.
128	      const fileKeyCopy = new Uint8Array(fileKey);
129	
130	      return {
131	        cid,
132	        encryptedSize,
133	        fileMetaIpnsName: fileMetaResult.fileMetaIpnsName,
134	        ipnsRecord: fileMetaResult.ipnsRecord,
135	        ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted: fileMetaResult.ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted,
136	        fileKey: fileKeyCopy,
137	      };
138	    } finally {
139	      // 6. Clear the internal copy of the key from memory
140	      clearBytes(fileKey);
141	    }
142	  });
143	}
144	
```

658	  async uploadFile(
659	    folderIpnsName: string,
660	    data: Uint8Array,
661	    fileName: string,
662	    mimeType: string,
663	    onProgress?: ProgressCallback
664	  ): Promise<{ cid: string }> {
665	    return this.withOperation('uploadFile', async () => {
666	      const folder = this.folderTree.get(folderIpnsName);
667	      if (!folder) throw new Error('Folder not loaded');
668	
669	      if (folder.children.some((child) => child.name === fileName)) {
670	        throw new Error('An item with this name already exists');
671	      }
672	
673	      const fileId = crypto.randomUUID();
674	
675	      // Build BYO-IPFS pinFn override when mode is not 'cipherbox'
676	      const mode = this.config.pinningConfig?.mode ?? 'cipherbox';
677	      let secondaryWarning: string | undefined;
678	      const pinFn =
679	        mode === 'cipherbox' || !this.externalProvider
680	          ? undefined
681	          : async (
682	              ctx: SdkContext,
683	              encData: Uint8Array,
684	              prog?: ProgressCallback
685	            ): Promise<{ cid: string; size: number }> => {
686	              const result = await this.pinWithMode(encData, ctx, prog);
687	              secondaryWarning = result.secondaryWarning;
688	              return { cid: result.cid, size: result.size };
689	            };
690	
691	      // 1. Encrypt and upload file, create file metadata
692	      const encryptionMode = selectEncryptionMode(mimeType, data.length);
693	      const uploadResult = await sdkCore.uploadFile({
694	        data,
695	        fileId,
696	        mimeType,
697	        folderKey: folder.folderKey,
698	        userPublicKey: this.config.vaultKeypair.publicKey,
699	        ctx: this.ctx,
700	        onProgress,
701	        teeKeys: this.config.teeKeys,
702	        pinFn,
703	        encryptionMode,
704	      });
705	
706	      try {
707	        // 2. Add FilePointer to folder's children
708	        const { updatedChildren } = sdkCore.addFilePointerToFolder({
709	          children: folder.children,
710	          fileId,
711	          fileName,
712	          fileMetaIpnsName: uploadResult.fileMetaIpnsName,
713	          ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted: uploadResult.ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted,
714	        });
715	
716	        // 3. Concurrent: file IPNS batch publish + folder metadata update
717	        //    These two operations are independent -- no data dependency between them.
718	        //    Using Promise.allSettled to handle partial failures gracefully.
719	        const [batchResult, folderResult] = await Promise.allSettled([
720	          sdkCore.batchPublishIpnsRecords([uploadResult.ipnsRecord], this.ctx),
721	          sdkCore.updateFolderMetadataAndPublish({
722	            children: updatedChildren,
723	            folderKey: folder.folderKey,
724	            ipnsPrivateKey: folder.ipnsKeypair.privateKey,
725	            ipnsName: folderIpnsName,
726	            sequenceNumber: folder.sequenceNumber,
727	            ctx: this.ctx,
728	          }),
729	        ]);
730	
731	        // Folder metadata update is critical -- must succeed for upload to be valid
732	        if (folderResult.status === 'rejected') {
733	          throw folderResult.reason;
734	        }
735	
736	        // File IPNS batch publish failure is non-critical -- the FilePointer in
737	        // folder metadata is valid, and the file IPNS record will be created
```

> AGENT

Here's the full picture. For **each file** in the sequential loop, the SDK's `client.uploadFile()` does:

1. **Encrypt + upload to IPFS** — the file content
2. **Create file IPNS metadata** — per-file metadata record
3. **`Promise.allSettled`** — two concurrent operations:
   - Batch publish the file's IPNS record
   - **`updateFolderMetadataAndPublish`** — updates the folder's children list and publishes the folder IPNS record

So **the folder IPNS entry is updated once per file** — N files = N folder metadata updates + N folder IPNS publishes. Each publish includes all previously-uploaded files plus the new one (it's an append-and-publish-whole-folder pattern).

There's no batching. If you drop 5 files:
- File 1: encrypt → IPFS → update folder metadata (1 child) → publish folder IPNS
- File 2: encrypt → IPFS → update folder metadata (2 children) → publish folder IPNS
- File 3: encrypt → IPFS → update folder metadata (3 children) → publish folder IPNS
- ...and so on

Each folder IPNS publish includes the full folder metadata with incrementing sequence numbers.

> DEVELOPER

for #2 there, surely you mean the File IPFS metadata above

> AGENT

You're right — step 2 creates the **file IPNS metadata record** (a per-file IPNS entry containing the CID, wrapped key, IV, size, mime type, etc.). It's not IPFS metadata — it's an IPNS-published record that the FilePointer in the folder references via `fileMetaIpnsName`.

So the per-file chain is:

1. Encrypt content + upload ciphertext to **IPFS** → get CID
2. Create **file IPNS metadata** (CID, wrapped key, IV, size, mime) → get `fileMetaIpnsName`
3. Add FilePointer (referencing `fileMetaIpnsName`) to folder children
4. Concurrently: publish file IPNS record + update & publish **folder IPNS** record

Two IPNS publishes per file — one for the file's own metadata, one for the folder.

> DEVELOPER

the FilePointer IPNS record points at a FileMetadata IPFS record though

> AGENT

You're right. The FilePointer in the folder references a `fileMetaIpnsName`, and that IPNS name resolves to an IPFS CID containing the FileMetadata (the encrypted JSON with CID, wrapped key, IV, size, mime type). So the chain is:

1. Encrypt content → upload ciphertext to **IPFS** → content CID
2. Create **FileMetadata** (content CID, wrapped key, IV, size, mime) → upload to **IPFS** → metadata CID → publish **IPNS** record pointing at that metadata CID
3. Add FilePointer (with `fileMetaIpnsName`) to folder children
4. Concurrently: publish file IPNS → metadata CID, update folder IPNS → folder metadata CID

The indirection is: FilePointer → IPNS name → resolves to → IPFS CID of FileMetadata → contains → IPFS CID of encrypted content.

> DEVELOPER

ok, so now that we have that flow correct, what happens when 5 files are uploaded to a folder at the same time by a user in the web app?

- How many IPFS pins
- How many IPNS publishes

Can any of these be batched or simplified - eg. only pinning new folder metadata once for the batch, and only publishing the folder ipns once.

> AGENT

**Current flow for 5 files (sequential):**

Per file: encrypt content → pin content → pin FileMetadata → publish file IPNS → update folder children → pin folder metadata → publish folder IPNS

| Operation | Per file | 5 files |
|-----------|----------|---------|
| IPFS pins (content) | 1 | 5 |
| IPFS pins (FileMetadata) | 1 | 5 |
| IPFS pins (folder metadata) | 1 | **5** |
| IPNS publishes (file) | 1 | 5 |
| IPNS publishes (folder) | 1 | **5** |
| **Total** | **5 ops** | **25 ops** |

The folder metadata is pinned and the folder IPNS is published 5 times — each time with one more FilePointer appended. Only the last one matters.

**Batched flow:**

Encrypt + pin all 5 files in parallel (steps 1-3 are independent), then update the folder once:

| Operation | 5 files |
|-----------|---------|
| IPFS pins (content) | 5 (parallel) |
| IPFS pins (FileMetadata) | 5 (parallel) |
| IPFS pins (folder metadata) | **1** |
| IPNS publishes (file) | **1 batch call** |
| IPNS publishes (folder) | **1** |
| **Total** | **13 ops** |

Saves 4 folder metadata pins, 4 folder IPNS publishes, and the file IPNS publishes collapse into 1 batch API call (the endpoint already supports arrays — the SDK passes `[oneRecord]` each time today). Plus the content/metadata work runs in parallel instead of sequential.

The SDK would need a `uploadFiles` (plural) method that does:
1. Parallel: encrypt + pin content + create FileMetadata for all files
2. Add all FilePointers to folder children at once
3. `batchPublishIpnsRecords(allFileRecords)` + `updateFolderMetadataAndPublish()` concurrently

[
  {
    "id": "PRRT_kwDOQ6DXvM53syI4",

      "nodes": [
        {
          "databaseId": 3010090581,
          "author": {
            "login": "copilot-pull-request-reviewer"
          },
          "body": "`actions.handleSync` is an async function that can throw (it rethrows during initial sync failures). `UploadZone` currently types `onUploadComplete` as `() => void` and invokes it without awaiting or error handling, so passing an async function here can lead to unhandled promise rejections and also bypasses the `useSyncPolling` wrapper (concurrency guard + sync status updates). Consider wrapping this call in a non-async handler that catches/logs errors, or adjusting `UploadZone` to accept/await a `Promise`-returning callback and route through the same sync orchestrator used by polling.\n```suggestion\n            <UploadZone\n              folderId={currentFolderId}\n              onUploadComplete={() => {\n                void actions.handleSync().catch((error) => {\n                  // Swallow sync errors from upload completion to avoid unhandled rejections.\n                  // Detailed handling is performed by the sync orchestration layer.\n                  console.error('Sync error after upload completion:', error);\n                });\n              }}\n            />\n```",
          "path": "apps/web/src/components/file-browser/FileBrowser.tsx",
          "line": 136
        }
      ]
    }
  }
]
```
